    Fix (#8781). sameValue treats a wanted null as equal to an absent field, as MongoDB does; matches evaluates the new empty-identity selector (BF-141) in memory, and hands any array comparison back to MongoDB.

    Reproduced 2026-09-26 with retry-keeps-srvcreated.js (Node 22.23.2, MongoDB 7.0.43 in a dedicated container; synthetic 20 g treatments; each arm writes once, reads the stored document, waits 20 ms and re-sends):

    arm v15.0.8 92d08342 aa1111b2 (#8775, not #8780) ff93fa94 (before #8781) ce7d754a (after)
    R1 v1 POST, no identity; identical re-POST 1 record, _id kept, no srvCreated kept srvCreated +41 ms, _id kept kept
    R2 the same, re-POST as [it] no srvCreated kept +31 ms kept
    R3 the same, re-POST as [it, a new one] no srvCreated kept +30 ms kept
    R4 carbs + insulin, no identity; re-POST no srvCreated kept +37 ms kept
    R5 with uuid; re-POST (control) no srvCreated kept kept kept
    R6 with identifier; re-POST (control) no srvCreated kept kept kept
    R7 v1 PUT of the document v1 GET returns no srvCreated kept kept kept
    R8 v1 PUT without srvCreated/srvModified no srvCreated kept kept kept
    R9 socket dbAdd identical re-send no srvCreated kept kept kept

    Every arm on every tree stores one record and keeps its _id; srvModified moves on every v1 re-send and not on the socket re-send, on all three dev trees. Probe exit 1 (a FAIL row on ff93fa94 only).

    Against #8781's description. It says retries "retain the original _id and srvCreated". In these arms the _id was never lost on ff93fa94; only srvCreated moved. A lost _id (a second record) is the empty-identity case, BF-141. It dates the regression to "after #8775 and #8780": it needs both, and aa1111b2 (after #8775, before #8780) does not show it.

    Reach (read). AndroidAPS maps srvCreated into its model; no use of it in a decision was found. The creation time is informational; history and sync use srvModified.

    BF-144 · on dev after #8775, a restart after a large import can hide new records from API v3 history — merged 2026-09-26 (PR #8781)¶

    Merged 2026-09-26 through PR #8781 by AndyLow91, an outside contributor (merge ce7d754a, fix a28fcecb); not released, goes into 15.0.9. Present on dev from #8775's merge 1157a8de to ce7d754a; never released.

    Mechanism. #8775 (BF-122) gave every server write a srvModified from one process-wide clock, srv-dates next(), which never returns a value twice and never goes back: two writes in the same millisecond get consecutive values, so a v1 batch of N documents ends about N ms ahead of wall time, and later writes continue from there. API v3 history returns records with srvModified > cursor, and a reader (AndroidAPS NSClientV3) takes the largest value it received as its next cursor. Within one process that is safe. But the clock was only a variable: after a restart it began again from wall time, which could still be behind the cursor a reader had taken from the batch. A record written then got a srvModified below that cursor, and history never returned it.

    Fix (#8781). At boot, before authorization, uploaders and the API start, the clock is set from the largest numeric srvModified stored in the six v3 collections (entries, treatments, devicestatus, profile, food, settings; configured collection names honoured; one indexed read per collection). A failure of that read is a boot error. Later writes continue above it.

    Reproduced 2026-09-26 with history-clock-restart.js (Node 22.23.2, MongoDB 7.0.43 in a dedicated container; synthetic sgv entries): 30,000 entries through v1 in three back-to-back requests of 10,000 (the v1 maximum), v3 entries history read to the end by ETag (limit=1000), SIGTERM, restart on the same database, one entry through v3 and one through v1, history read from the saved cursor.

    tree clock lead after the batch history read writes after restart, relative to the cursor history from the cursor
    v15.0.8 92d08342 none: v1 entries get no srvModified 0 records (v1 records are not in history, BF-122) v3 write at wall time v3 record only; not the defect's precondition
    aa1111b2 (#8775) 26,423 ms 30 pages, 30,000 records v3 −24,763 ms, v1 −24,755 ms 0 records
    ff93fa94 (before #8781) 25,201 ms 30 pages, 30,000 records v3 −23,553 ms, v1 −23,547 ms 0 records
    ce7d754a (after) 25,592 ms 30 pages, 30,000 records v3 +1 ms, v1 +2 ms both records

    Restarts took 0.8-1.0 s. The writes happened 23.6-24.8 s before wall time reached the cursor, so the window after such a restart is as long as the clock lead; a batch of 60,000 gave a lead of 53 s. Probe exit 1 (FAIL rows on aa1111b2 and ff93fa94).

    Against #8781's description. None found. Its reproduction (30,000 readings, all history pages, restart, a v3 write; history empty before, returning it after) is what the probe shows, and the v1 write behaves the same.

    Left (read). A reader whose cursor is ahead of every stored srvModified (a hard-deleted record's, or a clock rollback with a cursor from another server) is outside the restored value;

    8781 says it does not change the hard-delete and pre-commit history semantics.¶

    BF-145 · API v3 PATCH and PUT by the shown id miss a record whose identifier is null, "" or 0¶

    Mechanism. API v3 shows a record's _id as its identifier when the stored identifier is falsy (normalizeDoc, !doc.identifier). DELETE now finds such a record by that id (filterForEveryForm, BF-140 and BF-142). PATCH and PUT use a different helper, identifyingFilter (lib/api3/storage/mongoCollection/utils.js, used by modify.js), whose _id fallback applies only when the identifier field is absent. For a record stored with null, "" or 0, PATCH by the shown id matches nothing and answers 404; PUT matches nothing and inserts a new record, so two are stored.

    Measured 2026-09-26 by the BF-142 fix work on v15.0.8 92d08342, dev ce7d754a and 1c3aeb8c (Node 22.23.2, MongoDB 7.0.43): identical on all three, for null, "" and 0. Not a regression from 15.0.8.

    Reach. No client in the corpus is known to write these identifiers in normal use; AndroidAPS omits a null identifier and edits through PATCH by the identifier v3 gave it.

    Fix shape (not built). Give identifyingFilter the same falsy-identifier rule as filterForEveryForm, keeping BF-117's guard (a record with its own non-empty identifier is not reachable through its _id) and the requirement that _id equals a form of the requested id. Decide separately whether a PUT to such an id should replace the record.

    Decision 2026-09-26 (maintainer): file for after 15.0.9.

    BF-146 · API v3 treatments are held in memory without mills — merged 2026-09-26 (PR #8784)¶

    Merged 2026-09-26 through PR #8784 (merge ce30a94d, head d235bdf6; commits db99bba4 treatments and d235bdf6 device status); not released, goes into 15.0.9. RC run 020 on ce30a94d: 3473/0/3 in six cells (Node 20, 22, 24 × MongoDB 4.4, 7.0), the 15 new titles the only difference from 699eb5fa; tools/lab/rc-soak/probe-v3-late-mills.js exits 0 on ce30a94d and 1 on 699eb5fa and v15.0.8, where a late v3 bolus leaves IOB at 0, late v3 carbs leave COB at 0 and a late v3 device status is missing from the default v1 read, all three present after a restart.

    Mechanism. Every v1 write path in lib/server/treatments.js emits its documents to the server's cache through ddata.processRawDataForRuntime, which adds mills (from created_at) and endmills (from a duration). The API v3 storage wrapper (lib/api3/storage/mongoCachedCollection/index.js, updateInCache) emitted the stored document as it is. The cache keeps such a record (its age falls back to date). The dataloader builds the treatments from the cache and, once the cache holds enough treatments, refetches from MongoDB only its most recent minutes; a record inside that window comes back with mills and replaces the cached copy, a record dated before it does not. So a late v3 upload, or a v3 edit (PUT or PATCH) of an older record, is held without mills until restart or until it leaves the cache's retention.

    • lib/plugins/iob.js counts a treatment only when mills <= time and cob.js only when mills < time: the record counts in neither.
    • The dataloader sorts the treatments with a.mills - b.mills; with one record lacking mills the comparator returns NaN and the array is not in time order. cob.js takes "last carbs" in array order (BF-133) and chains each carb entry's decay on the one before it in array order, so an already absorbed entry processed after a newer one is counted again.
    • The page data the websocket sends to the browser is the same array, so the browser's own IOB and COB (the same plugins) are affected in the same way.

    Measured 2026-09-26 (Node 22.23.2, MongoDB 7.0.43, fresh test databases), with the branch's test file on three trees: v15.0.8 92d08342, dev 699eb5fa and bf/api3-cache-derived-fields db99bba4. On both unfixed trees, with enough treatments held for the incremental load: a late v3 bolus gives treatment IOB 0 and late v3 carbs COB 0 (the same on the page data sent over the socket, and after a v3 PATCH and PUT of an older bolus); with mixed v1 and v3 writes the loaded treatments come out in reverse time order; with an older (absorbed) and a newer carb entry and one late v3 record, "last carbs" names the older entry and COB is 34.5 g where the same records in time order give 10 g. A v1 bolus of the same age and a v3 bolus dated now are counted on every tree. On db99bba4 all nine tests pass.

    Other v3-cached collections (checked). The cache holds treatments, entries and device status. Entries: the dataloader builds each reading's mills from date, and v1's in-memory entries read sets mills from date; measured with a late v3 reading and more held readings than the default read, the reading is in its place in GET /api/v1/entries and in the page's glucose data, so entries are passed on as before. Device status: the dataloader adds mills to its own copy, so the pills and the page data are not affected; but GET /api/v1/devicestatus served from memory (no query other than count) sorts the cached records by mills (missing counts as 0), so a late v3 device status is placed after every record that has one. Measured on 699eb5fa and v15.0.8: with 24 held v1 statuses, a v3 status newer than all of them was missing from the default read; with the whole held set requested it came back last instead of first.

    Fix. The v3 wrapper passes treatments and device status through ddata.processRawDataForRuntime before emitting them, so they enter the cache exactly as a v1 write's do. Nothing stored changes: the helper derives on a copy (the v1 paths store no mills either). In GET /api/v1/treatments served from memory, a v3 record now carries mills (and endmills or a derived duration where the v1 helper adds them), as v1-written records there already do; its order is unchanged, since the cache already ordered it by date. In GET /api/v1/devicestatus served from memory, a v3 status carries mills and takes its place by time; the set of records for a given count is otherwise the same, and a late v3 status can now be inside the count where it was left out. Device status was added on 2026-09-26 (maintainer decision, for consistency with the v1 paths).

    Tests. tests/api3.cache-derived-fields.test.js, 15 tests on a real MongoDB. Red on 699eb5fa and v15.0.8 (10): a late v3 bolus in IOB, late v3 carbs in COB, a late v3 bolus after PATCH and PUT, the page data over the socket, time order with mixed v1 and v3 writes, "last carbs" after an edit (BF-133), the COB total against the time-ordered one; a late v3 status in the default v1 device status read, the whole held set read in time order, and the same with DENORMALIZE_DATES on (which rewrites created_at in place on the cached records; mills still matches the record's time over repeated reads). Green on all trees (5): a v1 bolus of the same age, a v3 bolus dated now, a v1 status of the same age in its place, a v3 status dated now first, and the page data holding the late v3 status with its time. Break-it: deriving the fields and then dropping mills gives the same red (7 for treatments, 3 for device status); deriving them only on create turns the PATCH/PUT test red; leaving device status out (db99bba4) gives the 3 device status tests red. Full suite 3473/0/3 on a fresh database (699eb5fa 3458/0/3; db99bba4 3467/0/3), no existing test changed. Treatment probes (same-time-carbs, same-time-resend-shapes, retry-keeps-srvcreated, v1-writes-v3-history) give the same output on 699eb5fa, db99bba4 and d235bdf6; v1-writes-v3-history exits 1 on its v1 DELETE arm on all of them.

    Not measured. Chart placement (the renderer uses a record's own date before mills), temporary basals from v3 in the basal display, and reports (which read MongoDB, not the cache). Older releases: the v3 wrapper emits the stored document since 14.1.0 (read, not run).

    BF-147 · two overrides hold ajv and request's form-data inside advisory ranges¶

    overrides["ajv@^6.0.0"] = "6.12.6" was set by 907fc5b4 ("Fix dev-mode bundle after PR #8518 merge: scope ajv overrides by version", 2026-05-14). GHSA-2g4f-4pwh-qvx6 (ReDoS with the $data option) was published on 2026-02-11 and ajv 6.14.0, its fix for the 6.x line, on 2026-02-20, so the value was inside the advisory's range when it was written. overrides.request["form-data"] = "2.5.5" came with 5ab0af7a (2026-05-10), when 2.5.5 was the newest 2.x; form-data 2.5.6 was released on 2026-06-12 and GHSA-hmw2-7cc7-3qxx (CRLF injection through multipart field names and filenames) was published on 2026-06-15, so this pin went stale after it was written. Both are on v15.0.8 and on dev 295f1177.

    Because overrides takes precedence over every declared range, npm audit cannot propose the patch releases: it reports form-data (and through it request, minimed-connect-to-nightscout and share2nightscout-bridge) as having no fix, and the ajv finding (with har-validator, schema-utils, style-loader, eslint and @eslint/eslintrc) as needing style-loader 4, a major. Nothing reports that the override is the cause. This is the BF-43 and BF-87 mechanism a third time.

    Reach. ajv 6 is loaded by webpack loader option validation (schema-utils), by eslint, and by har-validator inside request. The webpack schemas are the repository's own configuration. form-data 2.x is reached only through request, which only minimed-connect-to-nightscout (mmconnect) and share2nightscout-bridge load; in 15.0.9 the bridge runs only with DEXCOM_BRIDGE_USE_LEGACY=true (lib/server/bootevent.js, lib/server/bridge-connect-compat.js). Neither package builds a multipart body (read from node_modules/share2nightscout-bridge/index.js and node_modules/minimed-connect-to-nightscout/nightscout.js). Exploitability through Nightscout is not measured; severity low.

    Merged 2026-09-27 via PR #8786 (merge 7000eb18, 20:40Z; head 64a9cc13 on bf/override-advisory-pins, one commit on 295f1177). npm audit --package-lock-only on origin/dev 7000eb18: 7 (7 moderate), 5 with --omit=dev. The fix as run before the push: override values 6.14.0 and 2.5.6, then npm update browserslist baseline-browser-mapping postcss-selector-parser (their fixes are inside the declared ranges; the lockfile is stale, no override is involved). npm audit --package-lock-only goes from 17 (1 low, 13 moderate, 3 high) to 7 (7 moderate). Nine lockfile versions change, all patch or minor: ajv 6.12.6 → 6.14.0, request/node_modules/form-data 2.5.5 → 2.5.6, browserslist 4.28.2 → 4.29.1, baseline-browser-mapping 2.10.29 → 2.11.26, postcss-selector-parser 7.1.1 → 7.1.6, and caniuse-lite, electron-to-chromium, node-releases, update-browserslist-db with them. npm ci and the bundle build succeed (webpack 5.106.2, 3 warnings, as in RC runs 018 to 020); the full suite is 3473 passing, 0 failing, 3 pending on Node 24.15.0 with MongoDB 7.0.43 (a dedicated container, version read with db.version()), the same as RC run 020. Only one Node/MongoDB cell was run. The commit adds tests/dependency-overrides.test.js, which checks the resolved ajv 6 copies (under har-validator, style-loader's schema-utils and eslint) and request's form-data, and that form-data escapes line breaks in multipart field names and filenames: 5/5 on the branch, 0/5 on 295f1177's installed tree, each failing on the old version or the unescaped header. It was written after the suite run and was run on its own.

    On the modernization line the question disappears: rh/cut2 02205d91 has neither finding, and rh/cut4 135faa3b, rh/cut35 cd93d8e8 and cut 5 b1bdaca0 audit at 0 including dev dependencies.

    A gate that fails when an overrides value satisfies a published advisory range would have caught BF-87 and this entry; tools/qc/connector-pin-agreement-gate.js (which found BF-43) checks declared ranges only.

    3. How to use this register¶

    1. Anything found while doing multitenancy work that is also broken today gets an entry here, at the time it is found, with its evidence link. The register is the mechanism that keeps that promise.
    2. Prefer landing these independently. Each one is small, each ships to every current operator, and none needs a tenancy decision.
    3. Release-note the behaviour changes. BF-02 and BF-03 change what queries return. That is the point, and it should arrive as a documented fix. The note is in cgm-remote-monitor's CHANGELOG.md under [Unreleased] / Fixed, naming the collections and fields whose results change, with before/after examples.
    4. Keep severities honest. "Wrong answer with HTTP 200" is worse than "slow", and both are worse than "noisy". The table is sorted by that, not by effort.
    5. Allocate an id by reading the highest in §1/§1b at the moment you write it — never the one your brief quoted, because concurrent sessions file here.
    6. Check it is not a restatement before you file it. BF-12 is closed as invalid and is kept rather than deleted precisely because a deleted wrong entry gets raised again. Several of the entries filed on 2026-09-15 began as separate proposals and were merged into one, or folded into an existing entry as an amendment — the bulkUpsert scope findings amend BF-21 rather than taking ids, and three separate "everything says 15.0.9" proposals became one BF-60.
    7. Put it in §1 or §1b by the ships-to-operators-today test, not by how important it feels. §1 means present in what an operator runs today, which is master / 15.0.8 — not dev, not a cut branch, not an unmerged bf/* branch. That test is the only thing making §1 mean anything, and widening it would cost more than the entries it would admit.
    8. Say whether a prescribed fix has been run. An unrun prescription is marked UNVERIFIED in place rather than left to look settled.

    4. Where this file sits¶

    document relationship
    execution plan the reasoning, the decisions and the task definitions. Its §0 is the map of every other document
    queue/work-queue.yaml, queue/QUEUE.md, queue/README.md authoritative for item STATE, because its state is measured by a gate rather than asserted by an editor. This register is authoritative for defect facts and ids
    PR sequencing how the ten Phase 0 branches land
    maintainer release brief the same batch, presented as one decision
    semver and release versioning policy what version number each of these fixes forces

    One gap in that policy is worth knowing while reading this file. Its surface ladder has no question for what the client computes and shows a person, so BF-35 — the bolus calculator's quick-pick chooser resolving the wrong record, graded high here — classifies as a patch under it and carries no operator-facing note obligation at all. The HTTP bytes are identical and the defect is entirely in the browser. BF-36 is the same case. The policy document records this as an open decision rather than silently adopting a fix; it is flagged here so that a severity in this register is not quietly downgraded by a version number somewhere else.

    BF-87 · the qs override holds the connector below its range and pins the server's query parser¶

    overrides.qs = "6.15.1" and overrides.request.qs = "6.15.1" were added by 5ab0af7a ("Remediate runtime dependency CVEs", 2026-05-10), when 6.15.1 was the remedy. Since then the connector raised its own floor to ^6.15.3 and three advisories were published whose ranges include 6.15.1. overrides suppresses the ERESOLVE that would report the connector's range, so nothing says so — the BF-43 mechanism with a different package. Because the override is at the root, the single resolved copy is also the one express's and body-parser's query-string parsing uses, so the question is wider than the connector.

    Not reproduced as a runtime failure or an exploit. What is asserted is the silent range violation and the advisory ranges.

    Merged 2026-09-23 via PR #8749 (merge 9fd4600e; bf/qs-6.16 46b20b38): both override values are 6.16.0, the only version outside all three ranges. The documented query shapes parse identically and the suite is unchanged; only malformed bracket keys change. It changes the parser every request passes through.

    BF-88 · the modernization branch's TRUST_PROXY-unset default changes the client address in four cases¶

    395f3207 ("Restore proxy compatibility by default", lib/server/client-ip.js on origin/chore/nightscout-modernization b1bdaca0) does not reproduce the address forwarded-for gives on origin/dev 74fc6619 in four cases: an IPv6 entry after the first in a comma-and-space X-Forwarded-For chain; an IPv4 address with a non-numeric port suffix; a request with no socket remote address; and a request carrying more than one forwarding-header family (dev's precedence depends on which family earlier requests used; the branch's order is fixed).

    Reproduced by a differential probe. bf2/auth-hardening 8b975b41 pins dev's behaviour for the unset default, and restoring 395f3207's client-ip.js there fails exactly 7 of 48 tests/client-ip.test.js cases (re-run 2026-09-22). Severity: low — edge-case inputs, but the address is the failed-authentication delay's key and the setting is presented as compatibility-preserving, so an upgrader has no warning.

    Decided 2026-09-23 (maintainer): the cuts keep 15.0.9's unset default, forwarded-for as on dev; 395f3207's fixed-precedence normalisation is not taken, and cut 5's four tests/client-ip.test.js expectations are replaced by 15.0.9's when the cuts are rebased. No flag for the other normalisation: with TRUST_PROXY unset the forwarding headers come from any peer, so neither answer is a security boundary; an address list (proxy-addr) is the deterministic path. The unset path is revisited when TRUST_PROXY's default is flipped.
